Cause: downed.cpp re-hides the Keyblade every frame; the Keyblade's update **exe+0x3EFA60** re-shows a weapon that is away whenever it has a motion for the owner's current one (W_EX010 has 253; it has no 252, so lying is fine) and starts its **appear flash exe+0x3B6670(weapon, 0, 1, 0, 0) from exe+0x3EFB64** each time. Reproduced from outside (hide loop during 253 or idle: 378 KB). Hide handler vtable+0x80 = exe+0x3EF610 (only sets 0x700), show +0x88 = exe+0x3EF620. **Fix:** hook exe+0x3B6670; skip the call from 0x3EFB64 (return 0x3EFB69) for our Sora's weapons while g_weaponHold. With Cerberus' effects on top this is the likely cause of the 23:50:28 crash (1 s after the host's get-up).

Each game draws r in [0,1) from its own LCG (seed exe+0x783CA0 *= 69069) and gets Mickey if r < chance, decided in the Game Over ctor (object +0x14). Not checked yet: where n is increased (if only the Mickey game raises it, the two saves' chances drift apart). User idea: both roll; if both win, the bigger margin plays Mickey. Waiting for the user's design.

- **Timeline (scratchpad transition_watch.py, 1 ms polling, single copy):** at the transition NOW (exe+0x717008) and staging (0x717018) change and in-field (0x9BA8D0) goes 0 in the same ms; **NOW already has the final programs** (06/06 66/66/66; for the event's own move to 06/0F the request said 0xFFFF but NOW read 0/2/0 at once). Load 06/06: 1233 ms (in-field 0 -> 1), Sora actor +5 ms, event freeze flag (0x2A171E8 = 3) +8 ms after in-field 1. 06/0F: 293 ms. Event length 25.5 s.
- **Load code (Ghidra):** exe+0x152680 = the room-load task (one step per frame, FUN_150060(task,0) = wait a frame). It ends with `while (!FUN_156770()) wait;` then `FUN_1506B0(0, 1000, FUN_152CD0)`; FUN_156770 = `movzx eax, byte [exe+0x7177B8]; ret` (room ready flag; also read by 0x15F5F0, 0x3AC750, 0x3ACCF0, 0x3AD0E0, 0x3AD1C0); calls from the load task return to 0x1528E4 / 0x1528FF. FUN_152CD0 / 0x152F40 (both via function pointers) set in-field = 1 and start the room (camera, entities, event). In-field = 0 is written by 0x1524E0 and 0x152AE3.
- **Hold experiment (new src/dll/load_barrier.cpp, KH2COOP_LOAD_HOLD_MS, default off):** hook on 0x156770 answers "not ready" to the load task's two calls for N ms after the room is ready. With 3000 ms: 06/06 load took 4236 ms (= 1233 + 3000), screenshot during the hold = black (mean brightness 0.1), event freeze 6.6 ms after the release, cutscene played normally (Pete's line on screen) and lasted the same 25.5 s; the event's own move to 06/0F was held too (the real barrier must cover event-made moves). Research path: Volpestyle's lead (removal predicate exe+0x3DAC30) -> write watch on +0x120 (`KH2COOP_WATCH=120`) found the bit-28 "remove" write at exe+0x3B475E (exe+0x3B4700, via class slot +0x38 from exe+0x3BFF5C = the fade-done route: +0xA08 fade 1 -> 0, +0x9B8 bit 4) -> holding the fade didn't help (the script still sank them) -> **pinning the player pointer exe+0x2A105D0 to the copy for 12 s kept 4 Shadows the whole time, fighting**. The AI asks for the player through script command exe+0x42B230 (GetPlayer exe+0x3B3E80). A hook on exe+0x3B4420 (the script step) alone wasn't enough (it isn't the only VM entry). Fix (aggro.cpp, `AI_NEAREST_PLAYER`, default 1, compared in the settings handshake): for an enemy more than 1500 units from our Sora that is closer to the Sora copy (not downed), the player pointer is the copy during its actor update (puppet.cpp) and during every run of its AI script (hook on exe+0x41B400, owner actor = script thread +0x60). Near the host nothing changes (1500 limit). **Known limit:** while the friend is downed the swap is off on purpose (enemies leave a downed player), so for up to 30 s the far group can vanish and respawn again. The first runs looked half-working because the fighting Shadows downed the friend (28 HP).

## Session 17b (2026-10-08 15:50-16:12, user away; I worked alone): 5.10 load barrier built
User: "keep going with 5.10 while I'm away, then we will try this afternoon with my friend".
- **Built (load_barrier.cpp, replaces the KH2COOP_LOAD_HOLD_MS experiment):** `LOAD_BARRIER=1` (default on, both PCs, compared in the settings check; needs WORLD_SYNC), `LOAD_BARRIER_MS` (longest wait, default 5000). Host: when in-field drops to 0 with the friend in a room, it sends "KH2L" LOADING(seq, destination from NOW) every 3 frames; at its RoomReady (exe+0x156770, the load task's two calls) it holds until the friend's READY, a NACK, no answer within 1 s, or the time limit, then sends GO (30 frames). Friend: on LOADING it loads the room at once (follow.cpp `FollowLoadNow`, no 0.5 s settle) or sees it's already loading it (its own event / Continue) -> ACK; in a cutscene -> ACK, then NACK if its own event doesn't move it within 2 s; menu open etc. -> NACK; at its own RoomReady it holds and sends READY until GO (or the limit + 1 s). Load numbers start at a random value (a restarted host isn't mistaken for the old one).
- **Bench (two copies, 1 ms watcher on in-field and NOW of both games, scratchpad twatch.py), fade-in difference host -> friend:**
  - Merlin's house -> Borough (door): friend's load started 50 ms after the host's, friend ready first and waited 234 ms; both in **33 ms** apart (before the barrier: ~1-1.5 s, MODLOG session 12b).

  - Friend's menu open: NACK, the host went in without waiting (held 0 ms); the friend followed the normal way after closing its menu (4.5 s later).
  - Time limit: host LOAD_BARRIER_MS=100 and 700 ms simulated delay on the friend's packets: "time limit, going in alone; held 109 ms", the friend released on the host's GO, both in 18 ms apart.
  - Host loading the save from the title with the friend not in a room yet: "no waiting".

Before the barrier the friend was ~1 s behind, so its cutscene always ended after the host had moved. follow.cpp now keeps a refused move for 3 s and replays it when the host goes to that room (from the avatar packets or from LOADING). Benched: the friend walked into Merlin's door first (refused), the host followed 1.7 s later, the friend's own move was replayed and both came in 17 ms apart. Second Olympus run: clean (16 ms).

The commits were split per feature from the shared files (puppet.cpp, avatar_link.cpp, package_friend.py, start_host.bat) by staging intermediate versions.
- **Friend crash at the Cerberus event, cause found and fixed (follow.cpp, not committed yet):** the friend's cutscene ended ~1 s before the host's and asked for 06/07; the door lock refused it and kept it. When the host started loading 06/07, the host's avatar packet ("peer moved to 06/07") and the barrier's LOADING were handled in the same friend frame: the avatar packet replayed the refused move, then LOADING found the friend still in field and not "pending" and asked for 06/07 again. Two requests before the load began -> crash in the room teardown at exe+0x39DA55 (a resource released twice). Fix: every request goes through one function that remembers it until the load starts (in-field 0, or 2 s); nothing is added on top of one that hasn't started (FollowLoadNow answers "loading it" for the same room, the normal follow waits, a refused move isn't replayed). The refusal log now shows the refused move's programs and parameters. Bench: friend walked into Merlin's door first (refused), host followed; scratchpad freeze_on_load.py suspended the friend 120 ms the moment the host's load started, so both packets landed in one frame: "doing our own refused move now" + "loading it too" in the same ms, one request, no crash, fade-ins 24 ms apart. (Two earlier runs without the freeze got the packets 0 and 2 frames apart: also fine.)
- **Load barrier in the test (host log):** 15 loads; most released with "the friend is ready" (held 0-234 ms); "can't follow" 4 times (friend's pause; cutscenes whose own move didn't come within 2 s, e.g. 06/0F at 18:58, the Cerberus battle 06/07 at 19:10 while the restarted friend watched the cinematic); time limit once (19:09:06, the friend had crashed).

World_sync's TrackEnemy keyed entries by actor address and kept stale object/serial/spawn when the game reused the dead actor's memory within the 30-frame age-out. Fix: SameEnemy() (object, serial, dead->alive) else a new entry and netId. Repro tries: wait feature off, same path: no crash (group 244 came as serial 74); forced (Shadows at 1 HP, host moved into 244's box the moment a dead actor was disposed): no memory reuse; second forced try: the host wandered off. ## Session 19 (2026-10-09, midnight): boss reaction command result from the session 18b two-PC test

Some animations were off, e.g. Cerberus floating in the air while catching Sora. TODO 1.26 marked partly confirmed (host-pressed boss RC, Cerberus only); route checklist row updated. No code change.
- **User follow-up:** the floating Cerberus was seen only on the friend's game; on the host the camera was too close to Sora to tell, and it may be how the game shows it. **Q8 changed:** watching another player's boss RC becomes an option, on by default (on: the other player sees the RC's scene; off: it keeps moving and attacking, as now). Recorded in TODO Q8 and 1.26.
- **TODO 3.11 research (party HUD widget for the copy; user turned on the advisor for it):** RTTI names in the exe led to the HUD classes (`dk::GAUGE_PLAYER`, `dk::GAUGE_FRIEND`, `dk::FACE`). HUD init `exe+0x16C3A0` makes one player gauge and two friend gauges. Each gauge reads its actor from the array `exe+0xABCFE8` ([0] player, [1]/[2] friend gauges), set by `exe+0x168850(i, actor)`. AI party members put themselves in slot `[+0xDD0]+1`; the copy (player class, +0xDD0 = field command pointer) never gets a slot: that's why it has no widget. Only HUD code reads the array. The gauge's HP fill, damage trail and low-HP state all come from `*(actor+0x5C0)`, which for the copy is our own Sora's stat slot, so a plain insert would show our own HP. MP = stat block +0x180/+0x184. Face = "face" entries (types 0x18/0x19) of the actor's file at +0x928: Sora's `P_EX100.a.us` has them. All Ghidra only (VERIFIED_OFFSETS leads); next steps and the open HP question in TODO 3.11. No code change.
- **User picks for 3.11:** HP option (a) now (HUD shows the peer packet's HP); (b) (the copy's own stat block) later, "when we can fully focus on it"; MP stays full for now (later maybe the client sends MP as it casts). Do the bench tonight.
- **3.11 built + benched (`src/dll/party_hud.cpp`, new; `PeerPose.maxHp` added; UNCOMMITTED until the user's in-game OK):** each frame, 30 frames after the copy is alive it goes into the empty HUD list entry (`exe+0x168850`), and out again (`exe+0x168970`) when it's gone; the friend widget update `exe+0x182730` is hooked and runs with `copy+0x5C0` pointed at a stand-in stat block (peer HP/max HP from their packet, MP = max), restored right after; when the peer's HP changes the entry's event bit 4 is raised so the widget plays its HP-change/low-HP face. Two-copy bench (Merlin's House → HB 04/09, two runs): list read {our Sora, Goofy, empty} live, copy added at entry 2 on both, the game cleared the list on each room change and we re-added it, no crash; screenshots show Sora's face widget above Goofy's; damaging the host's Sora (debug command) to 3/28 drained the friend's copy widget while the friend's own gauge stayed on its own HP; first build without bit 4 kept the normal face, second build showed the red low-HP face. Damage on the friend's own Sora from the debug command is dropped there by design (host decides hits), so test damage on the host. Open: KO/greyed look while downed, Sora's face picture is bigger than the ring (cosmetic), MP. The host install now runs this build: a new friend package is needed before the next two-PC test.
- **User (looking at a bigger screenshot):** the widget looks fine, but Sora's face is drawn at the player gauge's size; asked for it at least half the size, picked option 1 (scaled copy of the face layout for the copy only).
- **Face size built + benched (party_hud.cpp, uncommitted):** hook `exe+0x3B57B0` (face lookup); only for the friend widget's call (`exe+0x17E9AC`) and the copy, the 2D layout (SEQD) is copied into our memory with every part quad scaled to COPY_HUD_FACE_SCALE % (default 50). First try: face missing; a 99% control run showed the same, and the user noticed the face only appeared once that Sora went low on HP. Cause: the animation groups are 0x24 bytes, not 0x18, so the copy cut off group 3 (the normal-state face). Fixed: half-size face inside the ring in the normal and the low-HP state on both copies. Note: I wrote two scratch .seqd files into the OpenKH extraction folder by mistake and deleted them at once (nothing else there touched).

## Session 22 (2026-10-10, ~01:50-02:20, you away; asked to keep digging why the cutscene twin doesn't move, TODO 5.12): found and fixed
- **Trace build** (`KH2COOP_TWIN_TRACE=1`, off by default, cutscene_twin.cpp): logs every SeqPlayAnimation / SeqActorPosition on a Sora actor or its twin with position, motion pointer and clock before and after the game's handler, and puts debug-register write watches (DR0..DR3, armed from a helper thread that suspends the game thread) on the twin's and Sora's motion pointer (`actor+0x170`) and position x (`actor+0x670`), counting each writer with its callers. First try (DLL 46506f17a824) crashed both copies with a stack overflow in clr.dll at the first hit: the hit lands on the scene walker's small fiber stack and the .NET runtime's exception handler ran before ours. Fixed by putting our handler first (re-added every 30 frames while armed); DLL ab064fc659ff ran clean.
- **Cause (mine, from an earlier slice):** besides the game's own writers, the twin had exactly one more: our puppet hook (PerEntityUpdate detour, called from `exe+0x3BF6C4`), every frame from the frame after the twin was made. The log said `puppet: Sora copy found (actor exe+0x2539C50)` = the twin, `held idle at (-2206, 0, 70)`, and the idle SetMotion cleared the twin's motion pointer (`exe+0x3C8AB2`). The puppet takes any updated player-class actor that isn't the list head as the other player's copy; during a story scene the field copy is gone, so it took the twin (P_EX120 is player class) and pinned it. It did the same in 707 and drove the actors of the next scene in 02/09 with the peer's pose.
- **Telling scene actors apart (read-only probe, scratchpad s22 `classes.py`):** `actor+0` holds a handle to the actor's class; every cutscene actor has `exe+0x752658` (set by `exe+0x419230`, the factory path for object | 0x40000000), the field Sora `exe+0x750300` (other classes in VERIFIED_OFFSETS 'Actor class handle'). Also seen: during 707 the list head `exe+0x2A171C8` is a PRIZE actor, which is what the puppet's 'the player switched actors' line at each scene start is.
- **Fix (puppet.cpp):** `IsSceneActor` (class `exe+0x752658`): never the copy; log line 'actor ... is a cutscene actor: not the copy' (each actor once, up to 16). DLL 6344fccd727b.
- **Fix bench (same route, both copies, trace on, scratchpad s21 `run4.sh`):** 706 twinned, the puppet skipped both scene Soras, the twin moved like Sora from frame 280 to 351 (+227/+83 against +226/+82, same motion object and clock) and took the frame-351 position; screenshots on both copies: the two Soras walk into the plaza side by side and take the battle stance together. The real copy was found again in 02/02, 02/06 and the Dusk fight. 707 twinned on both; screenshots: the two Soras stand beside each other with Donald and Goofy and walk off together. In close-ups only one Sora shows (the close-up twin is placed 90 units beside, not in those shots). No crash. Friend's 707 started 5.5 s before the host's again (TODO 5.14).
- **Also read in Ghidra** (VERIFIED_OFFSETS 'Cutscene twin shares Sora's animation entry' + lead): SeqPlayAnimation's entry lookup `exe+0x2CE760` returns the first entry in state 3; the twin's command right after Sora's gets the same entry (state 4 is set later by a task), seen live as the same motion pointer at every shared animation.

- **Friend crash after loading a save made in co-op (friend log kh2coop_22636, 17:14, friend alone, host not connected):** the friend converted the downloaded test saves with kh-pc-save-transfer, played file 8 (Roxas before Axel) on into Sora's Twilight Town and saved at the Usual Spot. The friend copied that sandbox file over the real save, loaded it, and opening the menu crashed at exe+0x36A4B2 (rax 7FFFFFE, rbx FFFFFFFF), the same crash as session 2. Cause (code + log): our party patch writes the copy into Donald's slot of the world's party entry in the game's save data in memory (save+0x3534 + 4*world, 0x00 = playable); a save writes that out. On load PatchPartyForClone found the slot already 0x00, returned without marking the world as patched (no 'party entry ... -> active' line in the log), so HookMenuPartyId didn't report the copy as Donald, and the menu read companion index -1. New TODO 5.19 (P0); VERIFIED_OFFSETS exe+0x3E60C0 row updated. Your KH2FM save on this PC was replaced at 17:19 with the converted test saves; the original is in the backup from 17:1x (`%USERPROFILE%\.universal-modder\backups\kh-saves\20261010-before-test-saves\`, same hash 4ca35cc6... as recorded in session 5).

- **5.19 part 1 (your pick: saves never carry the Sora copy):** coop_save.cpp hooks the game's save data write `exe+0x100340` (a co-op hook: on only while co-op is on). The game writes a staging copy of the save (KH2J, 0x10FC0 bytes) with a checksum at +8; the hook checks that checksum with the game's own CRC table (`exe+0xBEF898`; standard CRC-32 didn't match), copies the data, puts back the member the copy replaced in each world we patched (puppet.cpp `PuppetCleanSave`, also the bench), redoes the checksum and passes the clean copy to the game. If the checksum check ever fails the save is written as the game made it (logged). Items are {kind, message id}; kinds the game doesn't know go to a callback at def+0x28, so our own items can run our code. Item texts come from the message lookup `exe+0x3AD9A0(id)`, which we can hook to give our own words for new ids; the text format is decoded (A=0x2E, a=0x9A, space 0x01, '.' 0x4F). The list's +0x18 text line (shown under the title in mission pauses) can carry 'Connection lost. Reconnecting...' if we point its sprite at our text after the menu builds it.   - **TODO 6.3, lag test: found and fixed a world sync bug (uncommitted, world_sync.cpp).** Final Xemnas with `NET_SIM=150,30,2`: the friend's two Xemnas were bound and then removed 0.1 s later ('the host's enemy is gone') and never came back, so the friend fought nothing. Cause: with jitter the host's packets arrive out of order, and a late packet was still applied: its end loop dropped every entity whose newest sample was newer, and the next bind pass removed the friend's bosses for good. Fix: a host packet older than the newest one applied is dropped whole (also resets on a host restart); counted in the 10 s world line ('late host packets dropped'). Same lag test with the fix: both Xemnas bound and matched 2/2 for the whole fight, ~20 late packets/s dropped. (The host's effect pool still overflowed in that run, the open issue above.)

## Session 31 (2026-10-13, night, you asleep): a third party gauge for the copy (TODO 3.14)
- **Your request:** a third HUD gauge on the right, so Donald, Goofy and the Sora copy show at the same time; the copy's gauge should follow who is in the party (today it only shows when a Drive Form sends a companion away). Then P0 items from TODO if time is left.
- **Research (Ghidra, VERIFIED_OFFSETS row 'A third party gauge'):** the HUD (player gauge, two friend gauges, boss HP bar) is built at every room load and taken down before the next. Each gauge part registers itself as a 2D element with a 'group' id and gets its own HUD task, so a gauge set up the game's way is drawn every frame with no further call. The friend gauge's set-up takes an index: it moves the gauge up by its height x index and picks the list entry, flag word, face picture place and group from it. Index 2 is the free place above the two friend gauges, but its group would be 6, the boss HP bar's, and its list entry and flag word would fall past the end of the game's 3-entry list.
- **Built (party_hud.cpp, on by default, `COPY_HUD_THIRD=0` turns it off):** right after the game builds its HUD, a third friend gauge in our memory, set up at index 2 with its group base changed for that one call (group 7); hooks on the list getter and the flag test / clear answer its entry and flag word; the HUD's hide / pause bits are copied to it from friend gauge 1; it's destroyed right before the game's teardown. The copy shows there while both of the game's gauges are taken; when one frees up it moves into that one (the gauges stay packed) and back when the member returns.
- **Bench (two copies on your main monitor; screenshots in build/bench/extra_copy/hud_*.png, hud_sheet.png, hud2_sheet.png, summon_sheet.png):** HB 04/06 with Donald and Goofy: the copy's gauge above theirs in both games, small face, no other HUD picture damaged. Valor Form on the host: the copy moves into Goofy's gauge; Revert: back on top. Pause menu: dimmed with the others. The host at 30/60 HP: half ring on the friend's third gauge; the host downed: the red KO face there. Summon (Chicken Little) and Dismiss: the copy moves into a freed gauge and back. Boss fights (Beast's Castle ballroom, Twin Lords): the boss HP bar beside it; during Beast's Limit the copy moves into his gauge and back. Room changes (HUD rebuilt each time), Game Over, the friend leaving and rejoining: no crash.

The friend's game shows the host's values through the game's own setters (counter `exe+0x3FA230`, gauge `exe+0x3FA310`) when it runs the same mission in the same room, and moves its timer's start so the clock matches (more than 15 frames off). ## Session 41 (2026-10-23): research, each player's own Sora on the other screen (TODO 3.3, 3.4, 3.16)
- **Question (yours):** how the friend's Sora copy in the host's game (and the host's in the friend's) can carry its owner's own stats, abilities and Keyblade, beyond position, hits and HP.
- **Prior art:** Volpestyle's `docs/NATIVE_PRIVATE_STATUS.md` (pulled today) gives a native Sora clone its own stat sheet from the game's pool (`exe+0x3C03F0`) for one GoA setup; HP only, no gear, abilities or Keyblade, and it notes that a SAVE refresh (`exe+0x3C1D90`) and gear changes (`exe+0x3C1190`) are not handled. Nothing there on gear, abilities or a second Keyblade.
- **Ghidra (no bench, no game run):** the whole Sora stat sheet (HP, MP, AP, strength, magic, defense, resistances, the ability bits) is rebuilt by the game from Sora's 0x114-byte character record in the save, plus the form record and the gear's item rows (leads L121-L125). A hit's damage is computed from the victim's sheet when the hit is built (L126): in the host's game the copy shares the host's sheet, so the friend is hurt by the host's defense, armor, resistances and low-HP abilities, and the friend's game then only applies its own survive rules (1.75-1.77) to that number. The friend's own attacks already use the friend's stats (its game makes the claims, world_sync.cpp). The Keyblade a player body is built with is read from the save record, not the sheet (L127), so the copy always gets the local player's Keyblade.
- **Idea, not built:** give the copy its own pool sheet whose record, form record and Drive-gauge links point at buffers holding the partner's record (sent once and on change); the game's own code then computes everything. Open questions for you in this session's report.
